On 2026-09-17 — **the Cell Games**, first version — **SUPERSEDED the same day by the rebuild above; kept for history only** (the ten-phase mission, its scenes and its numbers no longer exist in the tree):

- **Chapter 6 · *The Cell Games*** (`src/data/missions/cell_games.js`, played as Goku and then as Gohan; ten phases). RING (scene `games_open`) → **GOKU** (checkpoint: a real fight against the perfect form that he is not allowed to finish) → FORFEIT (scene `goku_forfeits`) → **GOHAN** (checkpoint: an eleven-year-old holding back, which the mission carries as ×0.45 damage rather than as dialogue) → SIXTEEN (scene `sixteen_speaks`: the last thing 16 says, the music stopping, and two beats of a child shaking with the camera at 23°) → ASCEND (the Super Saiyan 2 transformation, entered from the story flag that scene sets — never from a clock) → **RAGE** (checkpoint: the payoff, played, at ×2.6 damage with Cell's regeneration switched off) → BEAM (scene `father_son`) → **END** (checkpoint: the only phase in the chapter where Cell can be killed) → AFTER (scene `cell_ends`).
- **Gohan at eleven is playable** (`src/data/characters/teen_gohan.js`, Level 1 prototype): his father's technique without his father's enjoyment — Masenko, Kamehameha and the **Father-Son Kamehameha** as an ultimate, with **three forms** (base → Super Saiyan → **Super Saiyan 2**, its own hair silhouette, its own lightning and a transformation nearly twice as long as the first). He shares no move id with Kid Gohan and keeps the same `hiddenPotential` mechanic he had at five.
- **Engine fixes found by building it:** a boss-derived mechanic on a character with no `boss` block (Android 16) crashed every frame — `Mechanics.boss` now tolerates that; missions can switch a regeneration off for a phase (`fighter.noRegen`); and a phase entered after a cutscene now always re-attaches player control, because the cutscene phases hand every fighter a scripted controller and the mission would otherwise sit there with nobody acting.

On 2026-09-17 — **Chapter 6 opens: Cell**:

- **Cell is three fighters in one body** (`src/data/characters/cell.js`, Level 1 prototype, story only), built the way Frieza's four forms were: **Imperfect** (hunched, spotted, long-limbed, a thief who would rather leave than fight), **Semi-Perfect** (bigger, heavier and slower for it) and **Perfect** (tighter, far faster, completely calm). Each form carries its own moveset, specials, multipliers, silhouette scale and aura, and the two transformations run through the shared `bossTransform` cinematic.
- **New mechanic: regeneration** (`Mechanics.bioRegen`). Leave him alone for three seconds and he grows the damage back at 3% of his health per second, so poking him from range is worth nothing and **pressure is the only thing that sticks**. It is announced on the HUD, and the first mission's optional goal is never letting him complete a regeneration.
- **Chapter 6 · *The Town That Stopped*** (`cell_first`, played as Piccolo): TOWN (scene `cell_sighting`: an empty street, and clothes with nobody in them) → TALK (scene `cell_names_it`) → FIGHT (checkpoint: the imperfect form, where the regeneration teaches itself) → GONE (scene `cell_escapes`: Solar Flare, and he is away) → AFTER.
- **Chapter 6 · *Seventeen*** (`cell_semi`, played as Piccolo, then as Tien): HOLD (checkpoint: forty seconds of keeping him off 17, which the story says you lose anyway) → TAKE (scene `cell_takes_17` and the staged transformation into the semi-perfect form) → STAND (scene `tien_holds_him`) → TIEN (checkpoint: control moves to Tien and the objective is **seconds, not damage**) → AFTER.
- **Chapter 6 · *Let Him Finish*** (`cell_perfect`, played as Vegeta): SEMI (checkpoint: a fight he is genuinely winning, at ×2.4 damage) → LET (scene `cell_takes_18`, then the transformation into the perfect form) → PERFECT (scene `perfect_arrives`) → BEATEN (checkpoint: the same man, ×0.5 damage, surviving the thing he asked for) → AFTER. The choice is in the cutscene and the consequence is a phase the player has to play.

On 2026-09-17 — **Chapter 5 continues: the Androids themselves**:

- **Chapter 5 · *Ten in the Morning*** (`src/data/missions/android_nineteen.js`, played as Goku and then as Vegeta). ARRIVE (scene `androids_arrive`) → GOKU (checkpoint: Super Saiyan Goku against Android 19, who cannot be finished here) → VIRUS (scenes `goku_virus` and `vegeta_arrives`: the fight stops because his heart does, and the man who turns up is the one nobody wanted to owe) → VEGETA (checkpoint: **control moves to another hero mid-mission**, and 19 can now be killed) → FLEE (scene `gero_flees`) → AFTER. The hand-off is the mission's structure rather than a cutscene: the player loses the first fight and finishes the second as somebody else.
- **New mechanic: energy absorption** (`Mechanics.absorber`). Every beam, blast or ki projectile that touches Android 19 or Dr. Gero is absorbed instead of landing: it **heals them** and **takes the ki the attacker spent**, and the engine announces it as an `absorbed` event. Strikes and throws are unaffected — that is the counterplay, and the mission teaches it with a prompt and an optional goal ("never feed him ki"). `Fighter._damageFrom` now lets a mechanic refuse a hit outright (a `damageIn` of 0 means no damage at all) and passes the attacker in, which is what makes the ki theft possible.
- **Chapter 5 · *The Pair on the Road*** (`src/data/missions/android_road.js`, played as Vegeta, then as Trunks). ROAD (scene `pair_on_the_road`) → VEGETA (checkpoint: the fight he waited three years for, which he is winning) → TURN (scene `eighteen_turns`: she stops pulling her strikes, and his arm goes) → TRUNKS (checkpoint: control moves again, and the objective becomes keeping her off his father for thirty seconds) → CALL (scene `seventeen_calls_it`) → AFTER. The loss is built out of what it costs him: his ki and stamina drain like a fighter's, and hers do not move at all.
- **Chapter 5 · *The Laboratory Door*** (`src/data/missions/android_lab.js`, played as Future Trunks). CHASE (checkpoint: fly the ridge to the laboratory, with the optional goal of doing it inside ten seconds) → DOOR (scene `gero_at_the_door`) → CAPSULES (checkpoint: the capsules are **stage props with health**, and every one broken is one fewer android later) → WAKE (scenes `seventeen_wakes` and `gero_ends`) → AFTER (scene `the_pair_leaves`). **There is no boss fight in it on purpose**: the worst thing that happens in this chapter is a switch being thrown, and the player is in the room for it.
- **New mechanic: infinite energy** (`Mechanics.tireless`). Androids 16, 17 and 18 hold their stamina full and their ki topped up, so outlasting them — chipping a guard, baiting a long charge, waiting out a ki bar — is not a plan. It changes nothing about the damage they take.
- **New characters (all Level 1 prototypes, story only):** **Android 19** (heavy, patient, palms), **Dr. Gero / Android 20** (thinner, quicker, would rather watch), **Android 17** (fast, bored, answers a crowd with an energy field), **Android 18** (faster still, answers it by getting closer, with a three-hit rush) and **Android 16** as a body in a capsule nobody opens. 17 and 18 share no special; 19 and 20 share none either.

On 2026-09-17 — **Chapter 5 opens: the Android saga begins**:

- **Chapter 5 · *What He Came To Say*** (first version — rebuilt 2026-09-24, see the top entry) (`src/data/missions/android_warning.js`, played as Goku). **Arrive** (scene `goku_arrives_late`: Instant Transmission, and nobody there knows who the boy is) → **Test** (checkpoint: the spar, and the only gameplay in the mission — neither fighter can die, Trunks fights as a Super Saiyan with his cuts pulled, and the objective is five clean hits) → **Truth** (scene `trunks_truth`: who he is, the two Androids, **May 12th, Age 767**, and the heart virus that kills Goku six months earlier) → **Leave** (scene `trunks_leaves`: the medicine, and he goes home) → **After** (the three-year clock starts). The chapter’s information is delivered as a conversation between two people standing in a field: no briefing screen, no exposition over black.

- **Chapter 5 · *The Stranger*** (first version — rebuilt 2026-09-24, see the top entry) (`src/data/missions/android_trunks.js`, played as Future Trunks). Age 764, on a new Earth stage. **Arrive** (scene `cold_arrival`: the ship lands, the soldiers fan out, and Frieza explains what he came for while somebody watches from the rocks) → **Soldiers** (checkpoint: the sword, in base form, against the landing party) → **Reveal** (scene `trunks_reveal`, and the answer to "what are you" is the transformation, staged through the shared cinematic) → **Frieza** (checkpoint: Super Saiyan Trunks against Mecha Frieza — a real boss fight the player finishes) → **Cut** (scene `frieza_cut_down`) → **Cold** (checkpoint: King Cold, bigger and slower) → **After** (scene `cold_falls`). Same rule as the Namek climax: every cinematic sits between two stretches of gameplay, and the canon gap is carried by mission multipliers rather than by the characters' own numbers.
- **Future Trunks is playable** (`src/data/characters/future_trunks.js`, Level 1 prototype): a swordsman who fights vertically — a diagonal cut, a return cut and a spin for the light chain, a rising blade and a two-handed drop for the heavies, **Burning Attack** (thrown, not charged), **Shining Sword Attack** (a four-hit rush) and **Heat Dome Attack**. He shares no move or special with the game's other blade (Yajirobe). His **Super Saiyan** is the same staged sequence every other Saiyan uses, with its own hair silhouette (`trunks`, `trunks_ssj`). Known simplification: the sword is always drawn, because the engine has no draw/sheathe state yet, so the scabbard on his back stays empty.
- **Mecha Frieza and King Cold** (`src/data/characters/cold_force.js`, Level 1 prototypes, story only). Mecha Frieza is a **separate fighter**, not the Namek boss with new numbers: he keeps Frieza's pose library (it is the same person), adds a mechanical arm sweep and a vent, and his two phases are ARROGANT and FURIOUS — he opens at range and empties everything once he is hurt. King Cold is the opposite: enormous reach, heavy slow strikes, phases AMUSED and OFFENDED. They share no special.
- **New stage: Coastal Flats** (`coast`, Earth, Age 764) — the wasteland builder with a cooler, darker palette than the Chapter 1 wasteland.
- **Two harness fixes found while verifying:** the encounter probe's human-like bot rolled on `Math.random()` instead of the seeded generator, which made one prologue readability test fail at random (it is deterministic now), and the subtitle speaker table had no entries for the new cast (Trunks was announced as "future_trunks"). Recoome, Burter, Jeice and Nail were missing too and are now named.
- **Tests: 365 → 372.**

On 2026-09-17 — **the Namek epilogue** (the chapter's last mission, built straight after the climax revision):

- **Chapter 4 · *What He Left Behind*** (`src/data/missions/namek_aftermath.js`, played as Goku; the last mission of the chapter). **Wishes** (scene `namek_wishes`: Porunga answers, the dead are returned, and everyone who can leave the planet leaves — the field empties around him one Namekian at a time) → **Search** (checkpoint: fly 114 m to Frieza's ship across ground that keeps erupting; the objective is a place, not a health bar, and the HUD marker points at a real object in the world) → **Ship** (scene `namek_ship_dead`: the hull is intact and the controls are dead) → **Pods** (checkpoint: reach a Ginyu pod before a 50-second clock, with the eruptions twice as frequent) → **Gone** (scene `namek_gone`: he gets in, the surface lets go, whiteout) → **After** (Earth is given the wrong answer — "he could not be wished back, which means he is not dead" — and then the right one is shown rather than said: a pod, climbing, alone against a dark sky).
- **There is no opponent in this mission.** It is the first mission in the game built entirely around navigation and a hazard: no enemy is spawned, Goku cannot be killed, and both objectives are distance and time. The beacons are stage props (`stage.addProp`), so the marker, the distance readout and the thing on screen are the same object.
- **Tests: 360 → 365.** The chapter now ends with this mission and is gated behind *Five Minutes*; it is played as Goku in the torn gi and Super Saiyan; a bot plays every phase, reaches both beacons inside the clock and closes the chapter; both runs are proven to be navigation with a live hazard (eruptions still hurt whoever stands on them, and Goku cannot be killed by them); and the wish scene ends in the same state watched or skipped.
- **Known limitation:** Frieza's ship and the Ginyu pod are the same round prototype hull at two sizes (Level 1 art). Nothing on Earth is shown — the Earth beat is a title card and two lines, because there is no Earth stage yet.

On 2026-09-17 — **the Namek climax revision** (the owner's note: the climax was still "a Frieza boss battle with some dialogue", and he wants to *play* Goku's fight with the scenes cut into it):

- **The climax mission is a fight with cinematics inside it** (`src/data/missions/namek_super_saiyan.js`). *The One He Made* now runs **BREAK** (scene `krillin_taken`) → **BEATEN** (checkpoint: a losing fight, in the ruined gi, against a Frieza who is already at 100%; he cannot be hurt here, and the point is what it costs) → **KRILLIN** (scene `frieza_krillin_line`) → **TRANSFORM** (scene `goku_super_saiyan`) → **PAY** (scene `goku_pays_for_that`) → **GOLD** (checkpoint: Super Saiyan against 100% Frieza — the payoff, played) → **DESPERATION** (checkpoint: he stops fighting to win and throws everything, until he breaks at 6%) → **AFTER**. **No cinematic is entered on a clock.** The Krillin line fires when the fight reaches its low point (Goku below 22% health); the transformation fires on the flag that line sets; the desperation phase fires on Frieza's own health.
- **The lines the owner specified, kept exactly** (`src/data/scenes/namek_rage.js`). Frieza: *"Fine. Have it your way. I'll just blast you into oblivion like I did that other Earthling."* — which sets the story flag `krillinMentionedByFrieza` — then Goku goes completely still, the music drops to nothing, the camera pushes in on his face while he shakes (three beats, 30° → 23° field of view), Frieza keeps talking because he has not understood, and then: *"DON'T YOU TALK ABOUT KRILLIN!"*. After the transformation, `goku_pays_for_that`: *"You monster, you'll pay for that!"*, and control returns.
- **Goku fights the second half in a ruined gi.** Battle damage is a **state**, not a form and not a new model: `Fighter.setTorn(on)` → `Model.setTorn(on)` swaps the gi torso meshes to skin, hides the trim and sleeves and adds a dirt tint; it combines with the base, Kaioken and Super Saiyan looks and **survives a transformation** (the engine re-applies it after a form's look). Scenes can set it with a `torn` beat, and the climax phases share one staging helper (`afterKrillin`), so entering any phase from the mission list looks the way it would if it had been played to.
- **Chapter 4 · *The Child*** (`src/data/missions/namek_gohan_frieza2.js`, played as Kid Gohan; between *The Emperor* and *Two More Shapes*). **Taunt** (scene: Frieza explains, pleasantly, the order he intends to kill them in, with Piccolo already down) → **Rage** (scene `gohan_rage`: stillness, shaking, the head coming up — and the scene itself turns on the state) → **Assault** (checkpoint: the rage is real gameplay — the fight is over when Frieza has lost 18% of his health, with landing a Masenko as the optional goal) → **Spent** (scene: what is left of him, and Krillin dragging him out) → **After**. The rage is the mechanic Gohan already owns (`Mechanics.hiddenPotential.unleash`), not a damage multiplier, and it is switched on in the scene's `end`, so skipping the scene lands in exactly the same state.
- **Frieza is finally beaten, and it happens in gameplay** (`src/data/missions/namek_end.js`). *Five Minutes* now runs CORE → COLLAPSE → BREAK → **FINISH** (checkpoint: the only phase in the chapter where `cannotDie` is off, fought while the ground keeps erupting under both of them) → **FALL** (cutscene: he goes down, and the planet does not care) → ESCAPE → AFTER. Goku wears the ruined gi for the whole mission.
- **Browser verification found four presentation bugs, all fixed:** the close-ups on Gohan cropped his head (a camera aimed at the chest is wrong for a child), the same for Goku's hair in the rage beats, the smoke in `frieza_survives` had cleared before the reveal (it is now emitted across two held beats so he comes out of it), and entering a climax phase directly used to show Goku in an intact gi with Krillin still standing on the field.
- **Tests: 343 → 360** (`node tests/run-tests.js` — 360 passed, 0 failed). Seventeen new tests cover *The Child* and its rage, the torn state surviving a transformation, the exact dialogue and its staging, the flag → transformation chain, the restructured phase order, every checkpoint handing back a playable state in the right form, the eruptions hurting Frieza as well as the player, one transformation and one transformation cinematic per run, no stale boss form or brain in the payoff fight, the HUD fields following the fighter the story hands you, and watched-versus-skipped parity for the Krillin line and the rage scene.

On 2026-09-16:

- **Cutscenes are data now** (`src/game/cinema.js`, scenes in `src/data/scenes/`). A scene is a storyboard — a list of beats, each carrying any of `cam` (position, look-at, fov, easing, orbit, offsets in the actor's own facing), `hold`, `pose`, `place`, `move`, `say`, `vfx`, `sfx`, `music`, `mood`, `shake`, `slow`, `screen`, `fade`, `title`, `hide`/`show`, `transform`, `flag` and a `call` escape hatch — plus an `end(ctx)` that declares the state the scene guarantees. `SL.Cinema.play(mission, sceneId)` runs it. **Skip safety is structural**: a skip stops the beat list and runs the same `end`, so a skipped scene and a watched scene leave identical state; the tests compare the two field by field.
- **Dialogue carries its original line and raises a voice cue** (`src/core/voice.js`). `mission.say(speaker, line, seconds, { jp, style, voice })` emits the written line, the Japanese where we have it, and a delivery hint; subtitles render the original above the translation (`audio.subtitleLanguage`: both / en / jp), and every line raises a cue through `SL.Voice`. **No recorded dialogue ships and none is downloaded**: `SL.Voice.provider` is the hook a local voice pack or a future original recording plugs into, and a provider that throws is ignored. Default `audio.voice` is `off`.
- **Goku's arrival is a staged scene, not a spawn** (`goku_namek_arrival`). Fourteen beats: the battlefield before the hero, the sound before the sight, the pod coming down with a crater and thrown rock, the steam clearing, four different reactions (Krillin, Gohan, Vegeta, Frieza), then a 33° close-up pushing in and the owner-supplied line — 「待たせたな…みんな」 with "Sorry to have kept you waiting... everyone." underneath — held for four seconds with the music off. Then he walks past them, the camera falls in behind him, and the player has control with Frieza already in front of them.
- **The Kaioken escalation is cinematic once, gameplay after** (`goku_kaioken_escalation`). Knocked down, steadied, the red rising with the ground breaking up and the camera circling, the burst, and Frieza noticing for the first time. The scene ends by unlocking and lighting the form; the checkpoint after it hands the same state to a retry. In gameplay the Kaioken is the engine's existing strain model — it burns health and builds strain while lit — and the mission's second half is built around paying for it.
- **Five climax missions, each shaped as cinematic → gameplay → cinematic.** *The One They Were Waiting For* (`namek_goku`: arrival, measuring him, the Kaioken, its price). *The Prince's Last Words* (`namek_vegeta_end`: hold Frieza off Vegeta, the beam fired past you, the death scene, and the burial — no music, no effects, a man digging). *Everything This Planet Has* (`namek_spirit_bomb`: the Spirit Bomb as a **played** fifteen-second charge with three allies buying time, interruptions costing real progress, then the impact, then four seconds of smoke before Frieza walks out of it). *The One He Made* (`namek_super_saiyan`: the breaking point staged with the camera on Goku's face and nothing shown, the longest transformation cinematic in the game, the gold half, a staged 100% power-up, and the survival half). *Five Minutes* (`namek_end`: the core shot, a fight on ground that erupts under both fighters, and a flight out while the planet goes).
- **Namek fights back.** The last mission's eruptions are real hazards on a warning ring: they damage and launch whoever is standing on them — Frieza included — and the air is the safe place, which is what the prompt teaches. The sky stays the colour of the core for the rest of the chapter (scenes can opt out of the runner's mood reset with `clearMood: false`).
- **Engine support added for all of it:** `fighter.specOverrides` lets a mission retune one move for one scene (the Spirit Bomb charge is 900 frames there instead of 260), `battle` scenes emit `sceneStart` / `sceneEnd`, and the transformation cinematic helper is now generic (`SL.data.cinematics.transform`) so a story grant (Piccolo taking Nail) and a boss transformation share one staging path.

- **Chapter 4 · *Two More Shapes*** (`namek_frieza2`, played as the fused Piccolo; thirteenth Namek mission). **Arrive** (cutscene: he lands carrying Nail, and Vegeta notices) → **Even** (checkpoint: the only fight on Namek so far where the player trades with Frieza as an equal — the mission carries that with ×1.6 damage and ×0.7 taken, because it is Nail's strength, not Piccolo's) → **Third** (staged transformation, witnessed by Piccolo) → **Outclassed** (checkpoint: 40 seconds of survival against something that is simply faster; the optional goal is landing one Special Beam Cannon on it) → **Final** (staged transformation, witnessed by Vegeta, who reads it exactly wrong: "he is making me a Super Saiyan") → **After**. Both mid-mission checkpoints stage the form the story left him in, so a retry never fights the wrong shape.

- **Frieza now has all four forms, and they are four different fighters.** Each form carries its own moveset, specials, multipliers, aura and silhouette: **First** (precision zoner: tail swipes, the guard-piercing Death Beam, Death Wave), **Second** (heavier and slower: long backswept horns, an impaling charge, the Death Storm eruption; ×1.45 damage, ×0.88 speed, ×1.35 defence), **Third** (the wrong shape moving too fast: elongated skull, a four-hit claw rush, a Death Beam barrage; ×1.3 speed), **Final** (small, smooth and far above the rest: **Death Saucers**, a fast Death Beam, the **Death Ball**; ×1.7 damage, ×1.45 speed). No two forms share a single move or special id.
- **One fight, four bodies.** His boss phases are the forms — FIRST FORM, SECOND FORM, THIRD FORM, FINAL FORM — and entering a phase plays the transformation that takes him into it (`bossTransform` techniques `toSecond`, `toThird`, `toFinal`). A mission holds him where the story needs him with `fighter.bossPhaseCap`, which is how *The Emperor* stays a first- and second-form fight even when his health says otherwise.
- **Transformation cinematics are a reusable system** (`src/data/missions/frieza_cinematics.js`, `SL.data.cinematics.transform` / `bossTransform`). A transformation is staged rather than assigned: a slow push-in with a line, then a low circling camera while the sky darkens toward the new aura's colour and the ground breaks up around him (rocks, dust, rising shake), then the burst — white flash, shockwave ring, explosion, camera trauma — then a reaction shot from whoever is standing in front of him, then control back. It is **skip-safe by construction**: a skip lands on exactly the same state (form applied, actors free, lighting and music restored), and both paths are tested against each other.
- **Frieza's mechanic is Cruelty, and his AI has a personality.** `Mechanics.frieza` inherits the boss phase controller and adds `def.cruelty`: **×1.35 damage against anyone already below 40% health** (announced once per exchange, and the HUD bar turns). `AIHooks.frieza` inherits the boss hook and adds the rest of him: he **punishes anyone who stops to charge** (patterns marked `punishCharge` score ×2.4), prefers **finishers** against a hurt opponent, backs off when crowded rather than trading, and loses patience as his own health goes.
- **`deathSaucer` technique** (schema type with `count`, `startup`, `interval`, `recovery`, `damage`, `speed`, `radius`, `life`, `seek`, `turnFrames`). The final form throws spinning discs that follow you instead of flying past; they cannot be guarded away. The canon detail is the counterplay: after `turnFrames` a saucer **stops taking sides** (`deathSaucerLoose`, team −1), so leading one back over its owner is a real tactic.
- **Forms can change shape, not just colour** (`src/game/model.js`). A form's `look` may now carry its own `headExtras` (horns, crests, antennae — rebuilt into their own group) and its own `scale`, composed on top of the character's base size. Frieza's forms grow, distort and then shrink again; the same support is what Cell and Buu will need later.
- **Nail** (`src/data/characters/namek_locals.js`), a real fighter rather than a cutscene body: 22,000 health, poise 70, long reach, the Namekian Wave, a Guardian Stand and a guardian barrage. His mechanic is new — **`Mechanics.regenerator`**: holding a guard knits him back together (`def.regen = { perSecond, maxFrac }`), up to 85% of his health and no further, which is the only reason a fight he cannot win is survivable.
- **Piccolo takes Nail.** Piccolo gained a second form (`nail`, "Fused": ×1.55 damage, ×1.2 speed, ×1.3 defence, its own aura and sequence). It is a **story grant**: marked `storyLocked`, so `Fighter` locks it at spawn and the skill wheel does not advertise a form the player has never heard of; *The Warrior* unlocks it with `m.teach(piccolo, 'form:nail')` and then stages the fusion through the shared transformation cinematic. The schema rule for form costs is now `noCost` — a form the player never buys (a boss enters it, or the story hands it over) does not need a ki price.
- **Chapter 4 · *The Warrior*** (`namek_nail`, played as Nail; eleventh Namek mission, between the frog and Frieza). **Stand** (cutscene: the meeting on the plateau) → **Stall** (checkpoint: 55 seconds of a fight you cannot win, where holding guard is what keeps you standing; optional goal — land eight hits anyway) → **Arm** (cutscene: Frieza takes an arm, it grows back, and Nail explains what the delay was for) → **Fusion** (cutscene: Piccolo lands and Nail offers him everything he has left) → **After**.
- **Chapter 4 · *The Emperor*** (`namek_frieza1`, played as Vegeta with Krillin and Gohan; twelfth Namek mission). **Arrival** (cutscene: he comes across the water, reads them with a scouter, and the camera puts all four of them in one frame) → **First form** (checkpoint: a real fight against a man who is not trying; the prompt teaches that his Death Beam goes through a guard) → **Second form** (the staged transformation) → **Hold** (checkpoint: survive 45 seconds of what came out of it, three heroes to switch between) → **After** (cutscene: "that was the second of four").
- **Three fragile bot runs fixed, found by the suite after the RNG draws shifted.** *The Hunted*'s hold now has Dodoria playing with the heroes rather than racing them (`damageDealtMul 0.6` in that phase; canon has both of them walk away), *The Captain*'s retake clock now runs whatever the thief is doing (he would otherwise never stop punching Vegeta long enough to reach for his body, and the phase could stall), and the Nappa duel bot now spends the Kaioken instead of saving it. The Nappa seed sweep was re-measured and the comment in the test updated: 8 of 12 seeds, losses all in the Goku duel.

- **Frieza stops being a prologue prop.** He was a seated cinematic body with placeholder numbers (99,999 health, no moves); he is now a **first-form boss**: 30,000 health, poise 90, a tail-led kit (a long tail swipe and a tail smash), the **Death Beam** (a fingertip shot that **goes through a guard**), the **Death Wave** and a **Death Beam Volley** ultimate, with phases **AMUSED** and **ANNOYED** — the wave and the volley belong to the second one. The prologue keeps working: the three pod poses were renamed (`fz_pod`, `fz_finger`, `fz_laugh`) and both prologue missions updated, so `idle` can be a standing stance. **The second, third and final forms are PLANNED, not built**, and he has no mission of his own yet.
- **Engine finding: fast thin projectiles could tunnel through fighters.** Collision is tested once per frame against the projectile position, so a blast moving 130 m/s steps 2.2 m between frames — further than the hit window around a fighter — and can pass straight through. Found while testing the Death Beam, whose speed is now capped at 82 with a slightly wider radius. Anything faster needs swept collision first (noted for the later Frieza forms).

- **Chapter 4 · *Nothing Like a Frog*** (`namek_frog`, played as Vegeta; tenth Namek mission). **Cleanup** (checkpoint): with nobody left to cover him, Jeice has only his spacing move, and the prince has a Zenkai (the canon gap is carried by the mission: ×1.9 damage dealt, ×0.5 taken). **Last try** (cutscene): the captain aims one final Body Change at Vegeta, and Goku throws the only thing within reach into the path — the same `swapBodies` call and the same interception rule as *The Captain*, with a frog on one end. **Armour** (cutscene): Vegeta helps himself to Ginyu Force armour and Goku names what is still out there. A new story-only character, the **Namekian Frog** (Level 1), exists only to be a body.

- **Captain Ginyu**, built as a fighter first so that the gimmick has something to sit on: 24,000 health, poise 72, a four-hit chain, the **Milky Cannon** (a beam), the **Captain Strike** (an armoured rush) and a **Captain Volley** ultimate. His phases are **CAPTAIN** and **COVETOUS** — and the Body Change pattern exists only in the second one, so he reaches for your body when the fight stops going his way rather than opening with it.
- **Body Change is a system, not an animation.** The `bodyChange` technique (`src/game/techniques.js`, schema type with `range`, `startup`, `recovery`, `failStagger`, `penalty`, `interceptRadius`) is a 78-frame telegraph — arms out, chest open, the scene dims — and then one decisive frame. It is armoured, so hitting him does not stop it; the counter is to not be there. **Dodging, vanishing, invulnerability or simply being out of range beat it; guarding does not**, because you cannot block a mind. A miss costs him a 96-frame stagger. And the beam takes whoever it reaches first: step into one aimed at somebody else (`interceptRadius`) and it takes you instead, which is how a body is ever won back.
- **`battle.swapBodies(a, b)`** does the swap itself. Everything physical stays with the body — health, ki, stats, moves, form, mechanic, cooldowns — and what moves is who is driving: **player control, the AI profile, the team, the party seat and the name on the HUD**. Each body carries a `mind` record (`{ id, name, ai, own }`), so the state is readable, testable and reversible. The details that make it safe: controllers are rebuilt rather than reused (a cached one holds the old profile), boss AI hooks are dropped when a mind moves into a body with no `def.boss` to read, every target that pointed at either body is re-resolved so nobody is left attacking their own side, the party seat is replaced rather than left dangling, and exactly one body is player-controlled at any moment. A stolen body carries a **×0.55 damage penalty** (the thief cannot use it properly), applied only to the body that was taken and removed when the mind leaves it.
- **Chapter 4 · *The Captain*** (`namek_ginyu`, played as Goku, and then as Goku in somebody else's body; ninth Namek mission). **Arrival** (cutscene): Jeice comes back with the captain, who reads Goku's power and decides he would rather own it. **Captain** (checkpoint): a straight fight until he is hurt enough to want the body; the optional goal is to never be standing there when the beam fires, and the prompt teaches it where it happens. **Change** (cutscene): he wrecks his own body first — so the trade is worth making — and takes Goku's at point-blank range. **Stolen** (checkpoint): the player fights on in the captain's ruined body (35% health, his moveset) with Krillin and Gohan, holding for 40 seconds. **Retake** (checkpoint): he goes for Vegeta instead, Vegeta keeps getting out of the way, and the job is to put yourself in the beam. **After** (cutscene). Reaching the *stolen* or *retake* checkpoint from the mission list stages the swap itself, so a retry is never stuck in the wrong body.

- **Burter — the fastest in the universe**, built so that the claim is a rule and not a stat line. 19,000 health and poise 50 (thin for a boss), the highest movement speed in the cast, and `def.boss.evade`: while he is genuinely moving — free, dashing, dodging, or in the travel frames of a rush, above 9 m/s — most of a hit slides off him (×0.35) and he leaves an afterimage. The moment he stops, he takes everything. His kit is a flurry rather than a haymaker: a four-hit chain that starts in three frames, the **Afterimage Strike** (a four-hit unarmoured rush), the **Mach Attack** (one enormous charge, second phase only) and **Blue Hurricane**. Phases **SPRINT** and **FRANTIC**.
- **`speedOrbit` technique** (`src/game/techniques.js`; schema type with `range`, `frames`, `radius`, `interval`, `damage`, `speed`, `recovery`). Blue Hurricane: he circles the target at speed and strikes from a new angle every `interval` frames, so blocking one direction is not an answer. The counter is the guard itself — **guarding a pass bounces him off it**: no damage, the technique ends immediately, and he lands in a 56-frame stagger. Ridden out instead, it is six passes and about 2,500 damage, and it still ends in a 46-frame recovery.
- **Speedster evasion is data, not a special case** (`Mechanics.boss.damageIn`, `def.boss.evade = { minSpeed, damageMul }`). Two details matter and both were found by testing: it reads live velocity rather than `fighter.speed` (that one only refreshes in the free state, so a stale reading would have kept him evasive through his own recovery — the entire punish window), and it is restricted to movement states, so a stagger, a guard break or a knockdown is never protected. Throws ignore it completely.
- **Jeice — the Red Magma**, deliberately the opposite of his partner: 20,000 health, 130 ki, preferred range 13 m against Burter's 2.5 m, and no move shared between them. The **Crusher Ball** punishes standing still, the **Purple Comet Volley** (a `scatter`) punishes walking in a straight line, **Crusher Volcano** is the ultimate, and when someone does get close he uses the **kick-off**: a new `retreatVolley` technique that throws him 15 m backwards over 42 frames while he fires three bolts on the way out. Phases **SPACING** and **CORNERED**.
- **Squad coordination** (`src/game/controllers.js`, `def.ai.squad = { role, holdRange, line }`, shared through `AIHooks.boss`). Two things only, and no multipliers anywhere: a pair stops piling onto one hero (each takes the nearest unclaimed one, refreshed five times a second), and each member has a role. **Pressure** (Burter) closes in, and presses harder while his partner is committed to a shot. **Space** (Jeice) holds his distance and **will not shoot through his own partner** — while Burter is in melee on the same hero, every ranged pattern and even loose ki blasts go quiet and he repositions instead, which makes standing next to Burter the quiet place on the field. Knock Burter down or kill him and the holding stops (and his ranged patterns gain 25%). Every change is emitted as `squadRole`, so it is legible from outside and testable.
- **Chapter 4 · *Two Against One*** (`namek_ginyu_pair`, played as Goku; eighth Namek mission). **Landing** (cutscene): Goku arrives, Recoome goes down in one blow, and the other two stop laughing. **Pair** (checkpoint): Goku against Burter and Jeice together — the goal is to put Burter down, with an optional goal of catching him three times while he is stopped; prompts teach the two rules where they happen (hit him when he has stopped, guard the Hurricane pass). Jeice cannot die here. **Down** (cutscene): Burter falls and Jeice finds himself alone. **Alone** (checkpoint): Jeice with nobody to cover him, fought until he breaks at 30% health; the optional goal is to take no Crusher Ball. **Flight** (cutscene): he leaves to fetch the captain, and Vegeta says what that means. Goku carries the canon gap in the mission rather than in the characters (×1.7 damage dealt, ×0.5 taken), so Burter and Jeice keep the numbers they were designed with and the Kaioken stays locked for Ginyu.
- **Damage events now name the special that caused them** (`battle.emit('damage', { …, source })`, fed by `projectile.name`). Missions could not previously tell "a Crusher Ball hit you" from "a ki blast hit you"; *Two Against One*'s optional goal needs exactly that.

- **Recoome — the second of the Ginyu Force**, and the opposite problem to Guldo: 26,000 health (the largest pool in the game so far, against Dodoria's 20,000), poise 80, and a wrestling kit — a guard-crushing lariat, a rising knee, a charging tackle and a suplex — plus the **Recoome Kick** (an armored rush), the **Recoome Eraser Gun** (a beam fired from his mouth) and **Recoome Boom** as his ultimate. His identity is not toughness, though: it is showmanship. His boss phases are **SHOWBOAT** (he keeps stopping to pose) and **SERIOUS** (he stops enjoying himself and stops posing).
- **`bossPose` technique** (`src/game/techniques.js`, schema type with `frames`, `buffFrames`, `damageBuff`, `breakDamage`). Recoome stops mid-fight to pose. It is a genuine opening — he does not guard and does not act — but if he is allowed to finish he comes out of it dealing **×1.25 damage for five seconds**, then it wears off. Damage absorbed during the pose above `breakDamage` (1,400) cancels it outright: no buff, and he staggers. His armour suppresses the *stagger*, not the damage, so poking does nothing and one committed heavy (or three solid hits) denies him.
- **Damage thresholds now survive story health floors.** `Fighter._applyDamage` accumulates `stats.damageTaken`, and `bossPose` measures absorbed damage rather than health lost. This was a real bug found by testing: missions give bosses `infiniteHp`/`cannotDie`, which floor health at 1, so a before/after health check could never fire — the pose-denial rule would have been silently dead in exactly the phase that needs it (*Recoome's Show*'s hold).
- **Chapter 4 · *Recoome's Show*** (`namek_recoome`, played as Vegeta and then as Krillin with Gohan; seventh Namek mission). **Challenge** (cutscene): Recoome asks for the fight and Vegeta gives it to him. **Pride** (checkpoint): Vegeta cannot out-muscle him, but every pose is a free opening — the objective is to punish three of them (optional goal: all three). **Show** (cutscene): the Recoome Kick flattens Vegeta, and Krillin and Gohan step in front of him. **Hold** (checkpoint): the player takes Krillin with Gohan as the second hero and survives 50 seconds; canon says nobody dies here, so both are `cannotDie` and Recoome cannot be killed. **Arrival** (cutscene): a pod comes down and Krillin recognises it.

- **Guldo — the first of the Ginyu Force** (`src/data/characters/ginyu_force.js`, Level 1 prototype, story only; the file is where Recoome, Burter, Jeice and Captain Ginyu will be added as the story reaches them). He is deliberately the weakest fighter on the field: 9,000 health against Dodoria's 20,000, light hits around 200 damage, and 130 ki — the largest pool of any enemy so far. His identity is the new **Time Freeze** technique: while he holds his breath the world goes grey and **every** enemy in range is locked in place with no way to mash out, which is the point — then the freeze ends and he is left gasping in a long stagger, which is the only real opening in the fight. He also carries the existing **telekinesis** technique as a Psychic Grip and hurls a torn-up boulder as his ultimate. Boss phases follow his nerve: **SMUG** (he plays with you) and **PANICKING** (he freezes nearly twice as often once he is hurt). Four eyes, squat body, Frieza Force armour.
- **`timeFreeze` technique** (`src/game/techniques.js`, schema type with `range`, `startup`, `holdFrames`, `kiPerSecond`, `recovery`). It puts every enemy inside its range into a held state for the duration, drains the user's ki each frame, tints the stage grey, and ends by releasing everyone and dropping the user into a stagger — on the timer, when the ki runs out, or if the state is left for any reason (the exit handler releases victims, so nobody can be stranded).
- **Chapter 4 · *Five Seconds*** (`namek_guldo`, played as Krillin with Kid Gohan as the second hero; sixth Namek mission). **Arrival** (cutscene): the squad lands, scouters read five power levels, and the smallest of them wins the right to fight the two Earthlings. **Guldo** (checkpoint): the fight, built around the frozen seconds rather than his fists — objectives reward never being caught in a freeze, and prompts teach punishing the gasp and switching heroes. **Breath**: his last freeze fails because he is out of air, and Vegeta, who has been watching, ends him. **After** (cutscene): Recoome is named as what comes next.

- **Chapter 4 · *The Eldest Namekian*** (`namek_guru`, played as Krillin; fifth Namek mission). Dende leads Krillin up to the Grand Elder while Frieza's search parties sweep the plateau. **Climb** (cutscene): Guru speaks, a scouter pings. **Patrol** (checkpoint): hold three soldiers off the elder (optional: Guru is never hit). **Awakening**: Guru lays a hand on Krillin's head — rings, streaming light, heartbeats, a burst — and the story *teaches* the form (`m.teach(krillin, 'form:potential')`), so it appears on the HUD and skill wheel instead of being announced in text; an elite soldier arrives immediately, tuned so base Krillin struggles and the new form does not. **Warning** (cutscene): Guru names what Frieza has called down — five more, not like the ones you have met. New story character **Grand Elder Guru** (Level 1, non-combatant, seated, nearly twice everyone else's height) in `src/data/characters/namek_locals.js`.
- **Krillin: Unlocked Potential** — a second form on his character (damage ×1.35, speed ×1.12, defence ×1.15, ki regen ×1.4, golden aura and a slight bulk/tint change). It is **story-locked** (`lockedForms: { potential: true }`) in the Namek missions before Guru, exactly the way Vegeta's Super Saiyan is held back, and opened by the new **`Mission.teach('form:<id>')`** path, which also clears the story lock on the transform button. In Free Battle he simply has it, the way Goku has Super Saiyan.

- **Chapter 4 · *The Prince Repaid*** (`namek_rematch`, played as Vegeta; fourth Namek mission, straight after *The Beautiful Monster*). The same opponent and the same two forms, with the roles reversed. **Wake** (cutscene): Vegeta at 8% health drags himself up and the **Zenkai** lands as an event — full health and ki, aura burst, shockwave ring, mood shift; mechanically it is a real `damageDealtMul` of 1.4, not a line of dialogue. **Rematch** (checkpoint): Zarbon opens *already* in his monstrous form (the fight starts where the last one ended), and this time his armored trades lose; goal is to break him to 25% (optional: inside 90 seconds, and never be knocked down). **Beg**: beaten out of the monster, he reverts to the elegant form and offers to lie to Frieza — and Vegeta answers with a blast. **End**: one Dragon Ball back, and the next target named. Story locks unchanged (no Super Saiyan, no Final Flash).

0. **Zarbon, a boss who changes the fight by changing form.** New character `src/data/characters/zarbon.js` (Level 1 prototype, story only) built as two genuinely different fighters sharing one body. **Normal form:** elegant and quick — a four-hit chain that reaches, a guard-crushing palm, a gliding knee, the homing **Elegant Blast**, **Mirage Step** (a teleport with fatigue: he blinks behind you or simply is not where you swung) and the **Sweeping Wave** ultimate; poise 55 against Dodoria's 90, because he is not built to trade. **Monstrous form:** slower (×0.86), heavier (damage ×1.45, defence ×1.32), bulk 1.34 with wilder hair, red eyes and a green tint — a short, armored three-hit chain, an armored slam and uppercut, a charging dash, the **Monster Crush** throw, a leaping **Pounce** (rush), the **Monster Quake** ground slam and the **Monster Wave** ultimate. His boss phases follow the forms (ELEGANT: mirage, ranged blast, taunt · MONSTROUS: pounce, quake, roar), so the AI's whole pattern set changes with him.
2. **Forms can now replace a moveset, not just multiply numbers.** `Fighter.moveSet` and `Fighter.specials()` resolve the active form's `moves`/`specials` over the character's own (the engine already did this for `form.moves.dash` and `form.moves.heavy` only), `specFor()` goes through them, and the AI's scoring, beam picking, throw reach and technique lookups read the active form instead of `def.specials`. A new **`bossTransform`** technique (`src/game/techniques.js`, schema type with a `form` index) hands a boss to the engine's own transform state, so a boss transformation plays the form's `sequence` — poses, aura, mood lighting, camera, burst — exactly like a player transformation, and returns control by itself.
3. **Chapter 4 · *The Beautiful Monster*** (`namek_zarbon`, played as Vegeta; third Namek mission, after *A Prince on Namek*). **Arrival** (cutscene): Vegeta counting stolen Dragon Balls, Zarbon drops in unhurried. **Duel** (checkpoint): the elegant form — the fight is about catching someone who keeps leaving; ends when he is broken to 45% (optional: break his composure twice). **Change**: he stops being beautiful — the transformation runs as a real event through `bossTransform`, with his line, the mood shift and Vegeta's reaction. **Monster** (checkpoint): survive 55 seconds of the transformed boss (optional: never be knocked down); canon says this is not the fight Vegeta wins, so Zarbon cannot be killed here and Vegeta cannot die. **Aftermath** (cutscene): one crushing blow, Vegeta left for dead by the water, Zarbon leaves with the Dragon Ball. Story locks unchanged (no Super Saiyan, no Final Flash). The rematch — where Vegeta returns the favour — is the next mission and does **not** exist yet.

1. **Bardock prologue ending cinematic** (`src/data/missions/prologue_ending.js`; new `ending` phase after the Supernova struggle in *The Final Stand*, not a checkpoint, so a retry replays the struggle that leads into it). Built only from engine systems (story camera shots, poses and faces, VFX, stage lighting and mood, synthesized audio, the music sequencer) — no text boxes, one whispered subtitle ("Kakarot..."). Eight beats, about 70 s: **1 defeat** — the Supernova swallows the Final Spirit Cannon, Bardock is thrown back and hangs in space hurt and barely floating (new pose `b_beaten`; no Defeated screen, no KO flow); **2 Frieza's attack** — Frieza raises his finger and the Supernova grows from 16 to 60 m over 9 s above him (it drifts toward Bardock so it never swallows its owner), lights the darkened scene and the planet below, with a flickering corona, embers and grit pulled in, crackling bolts, controlled rising shake, a rising charge loop and the Frieza theme climbing through its layers; **3 realization** — a last red flare of ki that sputters out (`b_defiant`), the fist opens (`b_realize`, new face `shock` then `calm`), the music fades to silence, an extreme close-up on his eyes, two heartbeats; **4 vision** — a push into his eye, then short dream flashes staged apart from the real scene (which is hidden meanwhile): Super Saiyan Goku striking Frieza, an energy wave, the two face to face, Goku's face in base form (the face Bardock recognizes); pale sky, soft halo, light roll on the camera, afterimages, a blurred and desaturated frame with glowing edges (`SL.ScreenFX.vision`, CSS on the canvas plus a `#vision` overlay), the whole mix muffled (`SL.Audio.setDream`, master low-pass) and a new bell-arpeggio track `vision`; a white flash returns to reality; **5 the smile** — back on his wide eyes, then a small quiet smile (`b_smile`, new face `soft` — **prototype approximation**: the procedural face has no smile shape, so eyes, brows and mouth are softened and the mouth lifts slightly) as a new hopeful choir track `farewell` begins, then he turns and reaches out toward the sphere (`b_reach`); **6 consumed** — the Supernova comes down on him: low shot from behind with his silhouette against it, the music at its climax, contact, whiteout, a fading afterimage, gone; **7 Planet Vegeta** — one continuous shot from behind Frieza's pod: the sphere dives into the planet, glowing cracks spread from the impact over the whole surface while it swells, eruptions, then the detonation (flash, fireballs, two shockwave rings, flying chunks, a fire cloud) and a slow pull-back past the laughing Frieza; **8 Goku's story** — fade, then Earth: a single pod streaks past the camera and burns into the atmosphere of a blue planet, a short stinger (`dawn`), a title card "Earth · Age 737", fade out. Timing runs on simulation ticks (it pauses with the game). Every beat is emitted as a `cinematicBeat` event. A skip request (the engine flag bots and tests use) jumps to a clean end state; presses in the first 1.5 s are ignored so mashing the struggle never skips it. Leaving mid-cinematic (quit, retry) runs `Mission.onDispose` cleanups that restore the screen filter, audio and scenery. Starting the mission at phase 6 stages the aftermath directly (for development and tests).
2. Chapter 4 continues in story order with **The Hunted** (`namek_hunted`, played as Krillin with Kid Gohan as the second hero; it now opens Chapter 4 and *A Prince on Namek* follows it). Canon beats: Frieza's men wipe out a Namekian village, Krillin and Gohan get the child **Dende** out of it, run with Dodoria hunting them by scouter, and only escape when Gohan blasts him in the face — after which Dodoria goes off and runs into Vegeta (the next mission). Phases: **Village** — the elder is killed in front of Dende, then a two-soldier fight with the child behind you (optional: Dende is never hit); **Flight** (checkpoint) — Krillin carries Dende while Dodoria sweeps overhead: reach three rock shelters, and staying in the open in his line of sight gets you spotted and shot at (optional: never be spotted; how often you were spotted feeds the mission score); **Cornered** (checkpoint) — Dodoria blocks the way and cannot be killed: break his guard and let Gohan's Masenko land twice, with prompts to switch hero and to fire (his body slam still has to be jumped); **Getaway** — Gohan's blast, the escape, Dodoria's threat. New story-only Namekians in `src/data/characters/namek_locals.js` (Level 1, non-combatants): **Dende** and a **Village Elder**. A bot plays it start to finish (soldiers cleared, three shelters reached, Dodoria held off, Dende never hit).
3. **Browser check of the ending (Claude, not human).** The Browser pane could not take screenshots (the page was not drawing), so frames were stepped by hand, the WebGL canvas was captured right after each render and saved through a loopback-only receiver in the session scratchpad, and the positions of Bardock, Frieza and the sphere were projected into each shot. That found and fixed: the vision actors were being pulled back into the 150 m orbit arena (the dream was empty), the camera ended up inside the detonation fireball (back faces culled, no fireball), the sphere's light overexposed faces to flat yellow, a faint aura rendered as a grey blob, Bardock was tiny or on the frame edge in two attack shots and hidden below the frame in the over-the-shoulder shot, Frieza was hidden by his pod rim, the core read as a flat disc, and debris flew through close-ups. The CSS dream filter and overlay are not part of canvas captures and were not seen.
4. **Documentation caught up with the Namek slice** (2026-09-15 work: *A Prince on Namek*, Cui, Dodoria, the Namek stage, the boss beam fix), which had not made it into these files.

On 2026-09-15:

1. **Namek foundation started** (chronological; built in the 0.3 folder and labelled "in development") — Chapter 4 *A Prince on Namek* (`namek_vegeta`, playable as Vegeta; opens after *The Saiyan Prince*): arrival cutscene → **Cui** duel (checkpoint; optional: never take the Cui Cannon; ends with Vegeta's "look behind you" trick) → **Dodoria** boss (checkpoint; optional: break his guard twice) → Dodoria, begging, reveals that Frieza destroyed Planet Vegeta (a callback to the Bardock prologue) and Vegeta finishes him. Story locks: no Super Saiyan, no Final Flash. New **Namek stage** (data only on the existing terrain builder: green sky, blue-green grass, rock towers, round blue Ajisa trees, lakes; unlocked by clearing the mission). New characters in `src/data/characters/namek_force.js` (Level 1, story only): **Cui** (ranged gunner rival: Energy Volley, Cui Beam, Cui Cannon) and **Dodoria** (boss on the Nappa framework: poise, telegraphed body slam, shoulder ram, bear hug, Mouth Energy Wave; phases Bully and Cornered). Careful bot clears it on 7 of 7 seeds.
2. **Bosses beam only through their phase patterns.** Found while testing Dodoria: the generic AI's `beam` score (and its combo-into-beam cancel) was never removed by the boss hook, so Nappa, Dodoria and the Great Apes could charge beams in phases with no beam pattern (Dodoria beamed Vegeta in the first second of the fight). Reactive counter-beams (beam clashes) stay. Nappa and What-If tests still pass; the Namek bot went from 5 of 7 to 7 of 7.
3. **Yamcha is playable** (unlocked by clearing *The Saibamen*) with the spec's **Spirit Ball Control**: after throwing the Spirit Ball, keeping Ultimate held puts him in a steering stance (`spiritSteer` state in `techniques.js`) — left/right on screen curves the ball (2.6 rad/s), Fly Up/Down lifts or drops it, forward/back changes its speed; releasing, 3 s passing or being hit ends control. A steered ball bursts through its first target (a push instead of a knockdown), rises for a moment and snaps straight back for a second hit at 60% damage (canon Sokidan). A CPU Yamcha's ball steers itself at the target for 1.5 s. Unsteered, it only homes lightly. Real keyboard steering checked in the browser by Claude (not human).
4. **Playable Great Ape Gohan** (the spec's "playable Great Ape sequence", in *The Saiyan Prince*). New story order after Vegeta shrinks: the Power Ball stays in the sky → *desperate* (Gohan and Krillin wear Vegeta down to 30%) → *moonlight* (Vegeta turns on Gohan; Gohan's tail is still there, he looks up at the Power Ball and grows) → **gohanApe** (checkpoint; the player IS the Great Ape) → Vegeta cuts the tail and the falling giant crushes him → *retreat*. Controls on the giant: Light swats, Heavy smashes, dash body-checks, Guard + Heavy grabs and crushes, Special 1 Stomp, Special 2 Mouth Blast, Ultimate Roar, skill wheel Arm Sweep; the techniques telegraph on the ground as they do for the boss and, from a button, cost ki and have cooldowns (`payFromSlot` in `techniques.js`; the boss AI is unchanged). The giant turns at most 1.3 rad/s (`def.turnSpeed`), the camera pull-back is capped for giant players. Vegeta (`tailHunter` controller) circles 19–24 m out firing blasts, notices telegraphs a moment late and not every time, and dives round the giant's side for the tail when it is busy or turned away; a solid hit sends him back. Goal: crush Vegeta (45% of his health in damage); optional: keep him off the tail, catch him in your hand. No fail state — the story ends the same either way (canon: Vegeta cuts the tail and the ape falls on him). Character `great_ape_gohan_player` (story only, Level 1). Tuned with bots: a player who stands still loses the tail in about 36 s; the rampage bot crushes Vegeta in 14–44 s (seeds 1–4) and kept the tail every time. New Kid Gohan poses `kg_lookUp`, `kg_sleep`.
5. **Snake Way** (`saiyan_snakeway`, new Chapter 2 mission between *Alone in the Wild* and *Ten Times Gravity*). Goku checks in with King Yemma (heard from his check-in station) and runs a narrow, winding serpent road over a sea of clouds to King Kai's planet, which hangs in the sky ahead as the landmark. He cannot fly yet and sprinting tires him, so the run must be paced; three short gaps (4, 5.5 and 6.5 m) must be jumped; five glowing road posts count the distance and light up as he passes; falling off drops him into the clouds and puts him back at the last post with 5 s added. Optional goals: never fall, reach the tail within 85 s. Goku wears the halo of the dead. New story-only stage `snakeway` (road sampled every 0.5 m with exact edges, `stage.roadInfo()`; not listed for Free Battle). Liberties: the road is compressed to 786 m and the gaps are not canon. Bot playthrough, fall/respawn test and road geometry test added; real keyboard jumps over all three gaps checked in the browser by Claude (not a human playtest).
6. **Boss attention in party battles** (`Battle.bossAttention`). Probing *Nappa Attacks* showed the hero the player controls took 87% of Nappa's damage while four CPU heroes stood by (the CPU target pick strongly prefers the player's hero). Now a boss fighting a party turns to another hero after a while on one (pressure rule `focus`: 7 s on Normal on the player's hero, half that on a CPU ally), turns at once to a hero who has hurt it much more lately, leaves a CPU ally below 35% health alone and goes back to the player's hero, and only ever turns between attacks. The party card of the hero a boss is after glows. In *What if Piccolo survived Nappa?* Kid Gohan now cannot be killed in the team fight (Nappa turns on him too, and the team attack needs him).
7. **Encounter probe** follows hero switches in party battles and reports how enemy damage is shared between the controlled hero and CPU allies; it used to keep measuring the first hero after a switch.
8. **Story lock fix:** a mission already cleared is never locked on the story screen (`SL.data.storyLocked`), so inserting Snake Way into the timeline cannot lock *Ten Times Gravity* for existing saves.
9. *Nappa Attacks* bot test re-seeded after boss attention (see *Latest Test Results*).

On 2026-09-14:

1. **Documentation for handoff** — created `MASTER_SPEC.md` (long-term specification extracted from the owner's original master prompt and later directives) and this `HANDOFF.md`; updated `STATUS.md` (automated vs human testing, playtest findings, checklists) and `README.md` (controls).
2. **Human playtest fix — flight descent.** Root cause: after being knocked back in the air only *Fly Up* allowed an early recovery (Fly Down was ignored for about 55 frames), the airborne recovery state ignored vertical input, dashes ignored vertical input, and on a controller descend was only on the left stick click (hard to hold while steering). Now Fly Down recovers exactly like Fly Up, recovery and knockdown honour up/down, dashes go up/down (straight vertical dashes allowed), and descend is on **C**, **left stick click** and **D-pad down**. Regression tests cover ascend, descend, boosted descend, vertical dash, forward, back, strafe, boost, guard-brake, downward dodge and the knockback recovery.
3. **Human playtest fix — lock-on / unlock / free aim.** New `src/game/targeting.js` (`SL.Targeting`): LOCKED ↔ FREE states, lockable objects (pods, destructibles, objectives) as well as fighters, flying enemies and bosses; **Tab / middle mouse** locks on or unlocks, **X / mouse wheel** (D-pad left, or a right-stick flick while locked, on a controller) switches targets; free aim fires blasts and beams through the screen centre with a 5° assist; a crosshair shows while free, an amber dashed ring marks a locked object. Target input is read before hit-stop so presses are never lost (this is why Tab seemed to do nothing). Pods can be locked from 220 m (fighters 90 m). **Unlocking now keeps the camera looking where it was**, so whatever was under the lock stays under the crosshair (before, the view swung up to 13° away). With *Auto Switch Target* off, a fallen locked enemy now frees the aim; before, the game quietly re-locked the next tick (found while writing these tests; not a human report).
4. **Human playtest fix — prologue encounter design** (not raw health). Measured first with a new tool, `tools/encounter_probe.js`. Added group pressure rules in `battle.js` (per-difficulty and per-mission limits on simultaneous melee attackers and ranged volleys, a minimum gap between attackers, and a grace period for enemies outside the camera view), telegraphed enemy volleys (a hand flash before the first shot, spaced shots), group-stun protection with a **breakaway** burst when several enemies stun-lock the player, and HUD threat arrows for off-screen enemies winding up. The prologue opening now spawns two soldiers in front of Bardock (a third after the first falls), the gunners got their own checkpoint and announce themselves, and pod reinforcements are capped at three enemies. Numbers are in *Latest Test Results*.
5. **Nappa bot test seed** re-picked after the group-pressure changes (the careful bot now wins 5 of 6 seeded runs; it was 4 of 6).
6. Earlier the same day: asset quality levels (`assetLevel` 1/2/3 with validation), Raditz Tail Grapple, Chiaotzu (Telekinesis, Psychic Link, Self-Destruct), Yajirobe (katana prop, Senzu Beans, sneak attacks), extra techniques beyond three buttons (`def.extras`, Explosive Demon Wave), Free Battle Form Level Limit, mouse pointing on the skill wheel, `.glb` base color textures and `tools/glb_check.js`, the Great Ape full-moon night in *Alone in the Wild*, Yajirobe cutting the Great Ape's tail, a regression sweep that starts every mission from every checkpoint.

## Current Playable Content

**Current Super additions:** Chapter 17 now contains The Thousand Soldiers → **Blue and Gold**; Chapter 18 Cabba and Chapter 19 End of Z remain. The older table below does not enumerate these newest additions; see the latest checkpoint and STATUS.

Everything below is a **functional prototype** unless noted. Nothing here is final art, animation or audio.

**Modes (main menu):** New Game / Story · Free Battle · Training · What If? (after Chapter 1) · Records (once there is progress) · Settings. There is no Tournament, Boss Rush, Gallery, Customization, Dragon Ball Hunt or Custom Character mode — and no buttons for them.

**Story (canon, in this build's numbering):**

> **Mapping to the master specification.** `MASTER_SPEC.md` §3 plans a five-mission Bardock prologue, then the original Dragon Ball (Kid Goku, World Tournament, King Piccolo, Piccolo Jr.) as Chapters 1–4 and the Saiyan Saga as Chapter 5. This build has one Bardock mission (covering roughly spec missions 3–5) and went from the prologue straight to the Saiyan Saga, as the owner's Version 0.2 brief directed ("the next major story content should naturally lead into the Saiyan-era story"). The original Dragon Ball chapters and spec prologue missions 1, 2 and 4 are **not built**. See *Current Remaining Tasks*.

**What-If:** *What if Goku defeated Raditz?* (`whatif_raditz`, after Chapter 1) · *What if Piccolo survived Nappa?* (`whatif_piccolo`, after *Nappa Attacks*). Saved on a separate What-If track.

**Playable characters (11):** Goku and Vegeta from the start; Bardock (Prologue); Raditz (Chapter 1); Kid Gohan (*Alone in the Wild*); Krillin, Tien and Yamcha (*The Saibamen*); Piccolo and Chiaotzu (*Nappa Attacks*); Yajirobe (*The Saiyan Prince*).

**Story-only characters:** Cui (Namek rival), Dodoria (Namek boss), Nappa (boss), Saibaman (enemy), Great Ape Vegeta and Great Ape Gohan (giant bosses), King Kai and Bubbles, Frieza (prologue), Frieza Force soldier, gunner and elite. `test_dummy` is a developer-only mannequin for the `.glb` pipeline (`playable: false`, so it never appears in character selection).

**Stages (7):** Rocky Wasteland (start), Planet Vegeta Orbit (Prologue), Grassland Hills (Chapter 1), Wilderness Canyon (*Alone in the Wild*), Snake Way (*Snake Way*; story only, never listed for Free Battle or Training), King Kai's Planet (*Ten Times Gravity*), Namek (*A Prince on Namek*).

**Bosses:** Dodoria (Namek; poise, telegraphed body slam, two phases), Raditz (story fight), Nappa (poise, telegraphs, three phases), Vegeta (multi-phase story fight), Great Ape Vegeta and Great Ape Gohan (giants with hit zones and a breakable tail), Frieza (prologue finale as a Supernova struggle — not a full multi-form boss fight).

**Transformations:** Goku — Kaioken (x2, x3, x4 levels with strain and overload; story caps it) and Super Saiyan (event sequence; locked in the Saiyan Saga story). Vegeta — Super Saiyan (Free Battle; not in the story yet). Great Ape growth for Vegeta and Gohan happens in story events; **the player controls Great Ape Gohan in *The Saiyan Prince*** (story only — no Great Ape in Free Battle or character select).

**Systems:** light/heavy strings with branches, launch and chase, throws and technique grabs, guard / Perfect Guard / guard break, dodge / vanish, combo burst, stamina, ki charge and blasts, beams with charge tiers, beam clashes (mash or hold), Spirit Bomb, ultimates, flight with ascend/descend/boost/dash, **lock-on / unlock / free aim**, **group pressure and breakaway**, **boss attention in party battles**, character mechanics (Saiyan Instinct, Pride, Battle-Hardened, Ruthless, Regeneration, Hidden Potential, Survivor, Third Eye, Psychic Link, Senzu Bean, Pack Hunter, boss poise, Spirit Ball Control), story-locked techniques and form caps, radial skill menu, party hero switching, boss framework (phases, patterns, poise, telegraphs), giant boss framework (hit zones, giant techniques, giant camera), technique registry, battle telemetry, `.glb` import pipeline (test mannequin only), checkpoints and retry, mission ranks and optional objectives, unlocks, save system with backup and corruption recovery, a separate What-If save track, settings with seven categories and full keyboard/controller rebinding.

**Not implemented (PLANNED):** Dragon Balls / Dragon Radar / Shenron, fusion, customization, custom character, tournaments, Boss Rush, gallery, secret menu progression, the original Dragon Ball chapters, most of Namek (only *A Prince on Namek* exists) and every later saga, a Great Ape outside the story (Free Battle), weather and time-of-day systems, desktop packaging.

## Current Character Asset Levels

1 = Functional Prototype · 2 = Polished Prototype · 3 = Production Asset. **Nothing is Level 3.** Every body is procedural (built in code). The validator in `src/data/schema.js` refuses Level 3 unless the body is a production `.glb` (`model.type: 'glb'` and `model.production: true`).

## Current Architecture

Plain HTML + CSS + JavaScript classic scripts (no modules, no build step, no fetch at runtime) so that double-clicking `index.html` works. One global namespace `SL`. three.js r149 is local in `vendor/`. Script order is in `index.html`.

| Area | Files | What lives there |
|---|---|---|
| Core | `src/core/namespace.js`, `settings.js`, `save.js`, `input.js`, `audio.js`, `music.js` | Version, settings schema (only settings some system reads) and presets, save slots with checksum/backup/corruption copy, keyboard/mouse/gamepad input with rebinding and mouse-wheel binds, synthesized SFX and music. |
| Rendering | `src/render/renderer.js`, `toon.js`, `camera.js`, `vfx.js`, `glb.js` | three.js renderer, cel shading + rim light + outlines, camera rig (lock-on follow, free orbit, cinematic shots, shake; keeps the view on unlock), effects, glTF 2.0 binary reader. |
| Fighters | `src/game/fighter.js`, `rules.js`, `techniques.js`, `mechanics.js`, `animator.js`, `model.js`, `model_glb.js`, `creature.js` | Shared combat state machine (moves, specials, forms, flight, reactions, group-stun protection), pure combat math, technique registry (latch, burrow, telekinesis, tail grapple, senzu, giant/boss techniques…), character mechanics, pose animation, procedural bodies, imported `.glb` bodies. |
| Control & AI | `src/game/controllers.js`, `targeting.js`, `radial.js`, `clash.js`, `projectiles.js` | Player controller and CPU AI (personalities, hooks, group-aware volleys and melee tokens), lock-on / free aim, skill wheel, beam clashes, blasts/beams/Spirit Bomb. |
| Battle & story | `src/game/battle.js`, `mission.js`, `stage.js`, `telemetry.js` | A fight (flow, targets, group pressure rules, boss attention, party switching, cinematic shots), mission runner (generator phases, checkpoints, objectives, prompts, spawners), stages with props and destruction (plus the Snake Way road with `roadInfo()`), per-battle statistics. |
| Data | `src/data/characters/*.js`, `missions/*.js`, `story.js`, `stages.js`, `unlocks.js`, `schema.js`, `dialogue.js`, `hair.js`, `poses_common.js` | Data-driven characters (validated by `schema.js`, including `assetLevel`), missions, canon timeline and What-If list, stages, unlock rules, lines, shared poses. |
| Wardrobe | `src/data/wardrobe.js`, `src/game/wardrobe.js`, `src/ui/screens_wardrobe.js`, eras in `src/data/story.js` | Outfit / armor / emblem / accessory pieces and per-character profiles (default look, canon look per era); `SL.Wardrobe` resolves what a fighter wears for the mode (canon story, What-If, Free Battle / Training / menus) and is applied once, in the `Fighter` constructor; the Customization screen. |
| UI | `src/ui/ui.js`, `hud.js`, `screens_menu.js`, `screens_select.js`, `screens_battle.js`, `screens_settings.js`, `css/game.css` | Screens, HUD (bars, lock ring, crosshair, threat arrows, party strip, skill panel, prompts; what shows when is Display & Immersion: `_wake`/`_visibility` in `hud.js`), settings screen generated from the schema, the first-launch visual setup (`SL.Screens.visualSetup`). |
| App | `src/main.js` | App loop, screen flow, launching battles and missions, crash recovery. |
| Assets | `assets/models/` (+ `README.md`), `vendor/three.min.js` | Only the original test mannequin (`test_dummy.glb` + `.glb.js`) and three.js (MIT). |
| Builds | `builds/v0.1/`, `builds/v0.2/` | Frozen, runnable copies. Do not edit. |
| Tests & tools | `tests/`, `tools/` | See *How To Test*. |

Adding a character: copy a character file in `src/data/characters/`, add a `<script>` line in `index.html`, an unlock rule in `src/data/unlocks.js`, then run the tests — the schema validator names what is missing (details in `README.md`).

## Current Controls

Every control is rebindable in **Settings → Controls**; in-game button hints show the current bindings.

| Action | Keyboard & mouse | Controller |
|---|---|---|
| Move | W A S D | Left stick |
| Camera | Mouse (click the game to capture it) | Right stick |
| Jump / Fly up | Space | A |
| Fly down / land | C | D-pad down, or left stick click |
| Boost (hold) / dash (tap with a direction; add Fly up/down for a vertical dash) | Left Shift | RT |
| Light attack | Left click or J | X |
| Heavy attack | Right click or K | Y |
| Ki blast (hold to charge) | E or L | B |
| Guard (also brakes in flight) | F or I | LT |
| Dodge / vanish (and burst free when stunned) | Q or U | RB |
| Charge ki | R or O | Hold LB |
| Special 1 / Special 2 / Ultimate | 1 / 2 / 3 | LB + X / LB + Y / LB + B |
| Transform / Power down | T / G | LB + A / LB + D-pad down |
| **Lock on / Unlock** | Tab or middle mouse button | Right stick click |
| **Next target** | X or mouse wheel down | D-pad left |
| **Previous target** | Mouse wheel up | — (not bound by default) |
| **Nearest target to the left / right** | — | Flick the right stick left / right while locked |
| Switch hero (party battles) | Z | D-pad right |
| Skill menu (hold, point, release) | V | D-pad up |
| Pause | Esc or P | Menu |
| Training: reset positions | Backspace | View |

While free-aiming (unlocked) a small crosshair sits at the screen centre; ki blasts and beams go there, snapping onto a lockable enemy or object within about 5°. A locked object (such as a pod) has an amber dashed ring. Enemies winding up outside the camera view show a pulsing arrow at the screen edge.

## How To Run

1. Open the `saiyan-legacy` folder.
2. Double-click **`index.html`** (Chrome or Edge recommended; Firefox works). No internet, install, server or build step is needed.
3. Press any key.

Optional development server (needed only for some browser tooling): `node tools/serve.js` then open `http://localhost:5173`. A Claude desktop launch configuration named `saiyan-legacy` (port 5173) exists in `.claude/launch.json` in the parent `coding` folder.

Frozen builds: `builds/v0.2/index.html` and `builds/v0.1/index.html`.

## How To Test

**Node tests** (need Node.js; players do not):

```bash
node tests/run-tests.js
```

About 30–45 minutes (the last full runs, 2026-10-11, took 32 and 44 minutes). Runs unit tests (combat rules, saves, settings, data integrity, schema of every character), every Combat Lab scenario headless, fighter-system tests, the human-playtest regression tests (flight, targeting, group pressure), per-character kits, boss and giant systems, telemetry, the `.glb` pipeline, battle rules, the radial menu, party battles, a regression sweep that starts every mission from every checkpoint, and story missions played by bots that only send player inputs (`tools/mission_brains.js`). Engine tests use fixed random seeds. `node tests/run-tests.js --fast` skips the engine tests.

**Combat Lab (in the browser):** open the game, press F12, run
`const s = document.createElement('script'); s.src = 'tools/lab.js'; document.body.appendChild(s);` then `SLLab.runAll().then(r => console.table(r))`. (The same scenarios also run headless inside the Node suite.)

**Balance duel (CPU vs CPU, telemetry):** `node tools/balance_duel.js goku vegeta 40 normal 1` — add `--tweak=noMech` (or `mechSwap`, `kitSwap`, `specialsSwap`, `statSwap`, `aiSwap`, `bodySwap`, `noIT`, `noKaioken`, `noForms`…) to remove one suspect at a time. CPU-vs-CPU finds broken behaviour; it is **not** human balance.

**Boss / swarm balance sims:** `node tools/balance_boss.js nappa goku 6 normal [cpu|reader]` · `node tools/balance_saibamen.js goku 3 6`.

**Encounter readability probe:** `node tools/encounter_probe.js <missionId> <startPhase> [seconds] [seed] [novice|average]` — plays a story phase with a simple human-like bot (4 runs; in party battles it follows hero switches and reports the share of enemy damage taken by the controlled hero versus CPU allies) and reports damage per 10 s, hits from outside the camera view, how many enemies hit within 2 s, the longest hit chain, enemy blasts per 10 s, time stunned and outcomes. Example: `node tools/encounter_probe.js prologue_final_stand 1 90 1 novice`.

**GLB check:** `node tools/glb_check.js model.glb [--write-js] [--id name] [--height 1.85] [--facing 180] [--map jointmap.json] [--json]` — checks and packages a real model (`assets/models/README.md`). `node tools/make_test_glb.js` regenerates the test mannequin.

**Browser checks:**
- `SL.dataProblems` in the console lists schema problems found at boot (empty = valid).
- `tools/viewer.html?char=ID&pose=stand` — model and pose viewer.
- `tools/snap.js` (load like the lab) — freeze moments and place the camera for visual checks.
- `tools/missionbot.js` (load like the lab) — `await MissionBot.run('saiyan_kingkai', { timeoutSec: 240 })` plays a mission with the bot brains.
- `SL.debugAutopilot = 'hard'` — the CPU plays the player's fighter.
- `SL.Telemetry.history()` — the last 40 battles in this browser.
- When a browser tab is hidden, `requestAnimationFrame` stops: drive frames manually (`SL.App.battle.update(1/60)` in a loop, then `render()`) and send real `KeyboardEvent`s to `window` to test input paths.

**Human playtesting:** use the checklists in `STATUS.md` → *Human playtest checklists*. Record what a person actually observed in `STATUS.md` → *Human playtest findings* and in *Human Playtest Findings* below — never mixed with automated results.

- **Full suite after pass 5:** `node tests/run-tests.js` — **724 passed, 0 failed** (715 before the pass: +7 in the new `tests/buu-ultimate-tests.js`, +2 checkpoint-sweep entries for `buu_zsword` and `buu_ultimate`; about 23 minutes). A first run after the pass: 721 passed, 3 failed — three older tests pinned adult Gohan's form list as "base, SSJ, SSJ2 (the Ultimate is a later milestone)"; they now check the four forms and that the Ultimate stays locked before *The Z Sword*. Doing that exposed a **real latent bug (Known Bugs #45, fixed)**: `Mission._lockUnearnedForms` wrote the story's "not earned yet" locks into `lockedForms`, and any mission that later reassigned `lockedForms = {...}` wiped them — in the Chapter 9 Dabura duel Gohan's lock on the new Ultimate was gone. The story locks now live in `Fighter.storyLocks` and a `lockedForms` accessor merges them into every assignment (`Mission.teach('form:x')` clears both); the updated Dabura assertion failed before the fix and passes after it. The run above is the clean run after the fix.
