### AUTOMATED TESTING (2026-09-17, after the Cell Games rebuild)

- **Full suite:** `node tests/run-tests.js` — **426 passed, 0 failed** (about 19 minutes). 410 before the rebuild: three outdated Cell Games tests were removed, **18 new ones** added in `tests/cell-games-tests.js`, and the story-order and checkpoint-sweep entries picked up the two new missions. The first run of the rebuilt tree found three real problems, all fixed before this run: the `beam` checkpoint started with no controllable player (Goku is dead by then, so the phase now hands control to Gohan before the prelude); a bot could not finish the chapter because the Father-Son Kamehameha was locked out of the Cell Junior fight as well (the slot now holds a Full-Power Kamehameha before the last stand instead of being locked); and *The Laboratory Door*'s story-order test still expected to follow *Ten in the Morning* directly, with *The Fever* now between them.
- **What the 18 Cell Games tests cover:** the arena as its own place (analytic platform, cracks instead of dents, crowd); 28 phases and 11 checkpoints with every scene played and no phase ending on a clock; all four Goku stages, including a Kamehameha at the planet that cannot be lost by ignoring it; Cell regrowing, the forfeit and the Senzu Bean; Gohan base → Super Saiyan → refusing; the Cell Junior squad (at most two on the hero, rotation, no chain-stun, allies really beaten); the rage staging and its composition with forms and the torn gi; watched-versus-skipped parity for the bed scene, Android 16's death and the Super Saiyan 2 change; Super Saiyan 2 as a kit and as a CPU personality; Cell adapting to it; the beam struggle with its beats in order, the clash won, Cell killed by the beam and all of his health accounted for as real damage; the struggle being unskippable and survivable; every checkpoint restoring its own story state; a bot playing the whole chapter; and the engine pieces (`Battle.on`/`off`, the story clash limit).
- **Browser (Claude — NOT human playtesting):** the whole chapter driven at `localhost:5173` with the battle paused at each beat — the arena, all four Goku stages (including Cell charging in the sky), the forfeit and the Senzu Bean, Gohan's path, the Juniors, **Android 16's death beat by beat with the rebuilt rage visual**, the Super Saiyan 2 reveal, the payoff fight, Cell's escalation and return, **the Father-Son Kamehameha** (both charges, the clash with the HUD, mash prompt and struggle bar readable, Goku's voice, Vegeta's blast, the clash won), Cell's defeat, the aftermath, ***The Fever*** in full, and a **checkpoint restart** through the pause menu's own path. **No console errors, no schema problems.** Thirteen problems were found there and fixed (listed under *Last Completed Work*). Two harness notes, not game bugs: `SL.debugSpeed = 0` does not stop the simulation (the main loop reads `SL.debugSpeed || 1`), so a capture harness must set `battle.paused`; and `tools/missionbot.js` cannot mash, so a browser bot always loses a beam struggle.

## Known Bugs

| # | Issue | State |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Flight: could ascend but not reliably descend (Bardock prologue). | **Fixed in code; verified by automated tests and Claude's browser input checks. Awaiting human retest.** |
| 2 | Prologue opening too punishing: surrounded, combined attacks, ki volleys, health vanished quickly. | **Fixed in code (encounter redesign, group pressure, breakaway, threat arrows); verified by the encounter probe and tests. Awaiting human retest** — especially whether enemies still feel dangerous. |
| 3 | Lock-on could not be released; could only switch between enemies; pods could not be aimed at. | **Fixed in code (targeting system, free aim, lockable pods, unlock keeps the view); verified by tests and browser input checks. Awaiting human retest.** |
| 4 | three.js warning `THREE.Material: 'map' parameter is undefined` repeats in the console. | Open, harmless; pre-existing (also in 0.2). |
| 5 | CPU Goku is weaker than CPU Vegeta and Bardock in CPU-vs-CPU duels (15/40, 9/40). | Open; may be CPU-only (Instant Transmission is worth less in CPU hands). Needs human play before tuning. |
| 6 | Free-aiming a pod from about 120 m takes many blasts (explosions at the pod's edge do reduced damage; locked blasts home in). | Observation, not confirmed as a problem; human to judge. Flying closer or locking on is much faster. |
| 7 | Browser: when the game tab is hidden, the simulation pauses (browser `requestAnimationFrame` throttling). | Expected browser behaviour; affects automated browser testing only. |
| 8 | *Auto Switch Target* off did not work for enemies: after a locked enemy fell, the game re-locked the next one anyway. | **Fixed** (found by Claude while writing tests); covered by an automated test. |
| 9 | In party battles a boss hunted only the hero the player controls (87% of Nappa's damage in the *Nappa Attacks* hold went to that hero while four CPU heroes stood by). Found with the encounter probe, not reported by a human. | **Fixed in code** (boss attention, 2026-09-15); automated tested; **not human playtested**. |
| 10 | The encounter probe kept measuring the first hero after a party switch. | **Fixed** (tool only). |
| 11 | Inserting a mission into the story timeline could lock an already cleared later mission for existing saves. | **Fixed** (`SL.data.storyLocked`), unit tested. |
| 12 | Bosses (Nappa, Dodoria, Great Apes) could charge beams in phases whose patterns have no beam (generic AI beam score and combo-into-beam cancel). Found by Claude while testing Dodoria. | **Fixed** (2026-09-15); automated tested. |
| 13 | Story cutscenes have no player-facing skip input: `battle.skipRequested` is only set by bots and tests (the intro and victory screens do have a skip). Found while building the prologue ending. | Open. The ending handles the flag safely; a real "hold to skip" for story cutscenes still needs building. |
| 14 | `Fighter` plays the sound cue `beamFire` when a beam leaves the hand, and that cue does not exist in `src/core/audio.js`. Every beam in the game is silent at that moment. Found while writing the Cell Games scenes (2026-09-17). | **Fixed** (2026-09-19, a synthesized `beamFire` cue). |
| 15 | `SL.App.startMission(id, { startPhase })` expects a phase **index**. Given a phase *id* (a string) it starts and immediately reports the mission complete instead of refusing. Found while driving the Cell Games from a phase in the browser (2026-09-17). | Open; only debug callers pass ids — the pause menu and the results screen pass indices. |
| 16 | `tools/missionbot.js` cannot mash: the headless harness fakes `SL.Input.pressed` during a beam clash, the browser bot does not, so a browser bot always loses a beam struggle. | Open, **tool limitation, not a game bug**. It means browser bot runs cannot judge clash balance; the headless tests do. |
| 36 | Cell Juniors: the "at most two Juniors in melee on Gohan at once" rule is broken on some seeds (3 at once). Measured by Claude on the code **before** the Chapter 10 pass: seeds 1 and 8 of 1–8 already broke it; the new forms shifted the seeded stream so the old test seed hit it (2026-09-24). Three squad-logic fixes in `src/game/controllers.js` made it no better and were reverted. **Root cause (found 2026-09-24, later):** the cap lived in melee tokens, but a swing started on an ally was turned onto Gohan when the squad plan moved its Junior or its ally went down, so the swing reached him without a token. | **Fixed** (2026-09-24): `Battle.squadMeleeFull` counts the squad's real swings at the player; enforced in `Fighter._startAttack`, `_tryChase` and, for turned swings, `AIHooks.cell_junior.pre`; circling Juniors give their token back. Seeds 1–8 all hold at two; the test is back on seed 6; regression test on seeds 1 and 6 (`tests/cell-purge-tests.js`). Squad members only. |
| 37 | `bomberWave` (Android 17's Energy Field, Android 16's Hell Flash Charge, Frieza's Death Wave as specials) cost no ki and set no cooldown when pressed from a technique slot, so it could be pressed again at once. Found by Claude building the Aura Burst (2026-09-24). | **Fixed** (it pays through `payFromSlot` like the other techniques; boss patterns unchanged); regression test. |
| 38 | `groundSlam` (Cell's Gravity Slam, Zarbon's Monster Quake as specials) has the same problem #37 had: free from a technique slot, no cooldown. Found by Claude alongside #37. | **Open** (not needed by the purge; one line — `payFromSlot` in its `start` — when someone plays those characters). |
| 39 | Stage props pushed any fighter out of their collision sphere, and the camera away from them, so a pilot could not sit inside the Time Machine (he ended up on its roof) and close cockpit shots were pushed back. Found by Claude in the browser (2026-09-25). | **Fixed** for scripted props: `prop.passable` skips both push-outs (the Time Machine sets it); every other prop still blocks. Regression test. |
| 41 | Explosions on a paved ring or a stadium's yard threw dust and debris in the landscape's grass colours (green rocks and a green haze on the white tournament ring). Found by Claude in the browser (2026-09-25). | **Fixed** at the root in `Stage.explode` (the arena's `dust`/`debris` colours when the point is on the ring or inside the stadium yard). |
| 42 | Fighter separation pushes scripted actors apart even when a scene holds them in contact on purpose (Goku was pushed 8 m back while Goten was held in his arms). Found by Claude in the browser (2026-09-25). | **Handled per beat**: close-contact beats set `intangible` on their actors and restore it afterwards (the hug, the sleeper hold, the pin, the full nelson, the palms). Open as a design note: any new close-contact scene must do the same. |

## Asset Blockers

**BLOCKED BY ASSETS** means code alone cannot produce it at good quality; it is different from **not implemented** (which code can build).

| Blocked by assets | Why | Current stand-in |
|---|---|---|
| Production 3D character models (Level 3) | Sculpted faces, muscles, clothing folds, hair volume and textures need a 3D artist. | Level 1–2 procedural prototype bodies. The `.glb` pipeline is ready to import them. |
| Hand-keyed or motion-captured animation | Weight, overlap and timing at anime quality need an animator. | Pose-to-pose animation with springs and squash/stretch. |
| Facial rigs / morph targets | Need model-side setup. | Procedural faces with eleven expressions (procedural bodies only); the quiet smile in the prologue ending is an approximation. |
| Voice acting | Needs actors; must never be ripped from the anime or games. | Subtitle-only lines. |
| Composed music | Needs a composer. | Synthesized music in code. |
| Recorded sound design | Needs a sound designer. | Synthesized SFX in code. |
| Detailed environments (cities, Kame House, Namek) | Need environment art. | Procedural terrain, rocks, trees, sky moods. |
| Artwork for gallery, loading screens, portraits | Needs illustrators. | None (no gallery exists). |

**Not implemented (buildable in code, not blocked):** Dragon Balls and Shenron, fusion, the custom character (the customization foundation exists since 2026-09-24), tournaments, Boss Rush, gallery screens, secret menu progression, original Dragon Ball chapters, Namek onward, playable Great Ape, weather, time of day, desktop packaging.

No outside asset has been downloaded or used except three.js r149 (approved; MIT licence in `vendor/three.LICENSE.txt`).

## Current GLB Pipeline

**FUNCTIONAL PROTOTYPE — proven with an original test mannequin only; no real character model exists yet.**

- **Works:** in-house glTF 2.0 binary reader (`src/render/glb.js`); skinned meshes, materials, embedded base color textures, skins, animation clips; name-based or explicit joint mapping to the 17-joint rig; every game pose retargeted onto the file's bones (`src/game/model_glb.js`); sockets for beams, props and hit sparks; outlines, rim light, hit flash, afterimages, HUD portrait; base64 `.glb.js` twin for `file://`; checker and packager `tools/glb_check.js`; verified in battle at 60 fps with two imported fighters.
- **Not supported yet:** morph targets (faces), per-form hair swaps (Super Saiyan), hand shapes, stretching limbs, cloth springs on imported bodies, normal/emissive/metal-roughness maps.
- **To add a real model:** follow `assets/models/README.md` (check → `--write-js` → `<script>` in `index.html` → `model: { type: 'glb', asset, height, jointMap, production: true }` → raise `assetLevel` to 3 → viewer → tests). Only with a model the owner made, commissioned or licensed, and explicitly approved.

### P1 — current version (0.3)
- Human playtest of the rest of 0.3 (Chapters 1–3, both What-Ifs, new characters, bosses, radial menu, Kaioken, party switching), one system at a time with the checklists; record findings honestly.

Checklists in `STATUS.md`.
- Group fights, measured 2026-09-15: *The Saibamen* pack shows no dogpiling (at most 2 enemies hit within 2 s, no off-screen hits); the problem in *Nappa Attacks* was boss focus, now handled by boss attention. The probe bots still usually lose the hold (a careful telegraph-reading bot wins it), so human play must decide whether it needs tuning. The Great Ape phase of *The Saiyan Prince* has not been probed in detail.
- Human playtest of the played Great Ape Gohan phase in *The Saiyan Prince* (does the giant feel heavy but controllable, is the camera readable, is keeping Vegeta off the tail fair, is 45% of his health the right goal). Tuning so far comes from bots only.
- Human playtest of playable Yamcha and Spirit Ball Control (is steering readable and worth the risk of standing still, does the second pass feel earned).
- **Human playtest of Chapters 5 and 6** (2026-09-17). Nothing in either has been played by a person. The questions bots cannot answer: does the absorption rule teach itself (do you work out that ki feeds 19 before the objective tells you); does Cell's regeneration make the fight tense or tedious; does the hand-off from Goku to Vegeta, and from Vegeta to Trunks, feel like the story or like losing control; is a boy holding back readable as a choice rather than as bad balance; and does the Super Saiyan 2 ascension land after the quiet beat that sets it up.
- **Human playtest of the whole Namek chapter, and of the climax in particular** (2026-09-17). Nothing in Chapter 4 has been played by a person. The questions bots cannot answer: does the losing fight before the Krillin line read as a story beat or as an unfair fight; does the line land with the silence and the push-in; is the Super Saiyan payoff satisfying to play rather than to watch; is 100% Frieza readable; does Gohan's rage in *The Child* feel like a child snapping; do the eruptions in *Five Minutes* feel like a dying planet or like a nuisance; and do the checkpoints resume where a player expects them to.
- **Human playtest of Display & Immersion** (2026-09-23): does Cinematic feel less cluttered and still readable in real fights; checklist in `STATUS.md`. Tune the presets (`SL.Settings.HUD_PRESETS`) and the wake reasons in `hud.js` from what a person reports, not from tests.

Do not build it, create placeholder chapters for it or restructure the campaign around it before then. The remaining Bardock prologue missions (spec missions 1, 2, 4) belong to the same pass.

- **Human playtests waiting**, oldest first: the Bardock prologue and its ending (now with the upgraded death); the core-feel pass (camera, lock-on, flight, the moon, the Raditz duel, charge audio, the Father-Son Kamehameha); *Snake Way* and boss attention; the rebuilt Cell Games (above all the Father-Son Kamehameha struggle); *The Fever*; the three Buu Milestone A missions, and the eight Milestone B missions (checklists in `STATUS.md`).

**How to resume:** read root CLAUDE.md, the newest HANDOFF checkpoint / Immediate Next Task and the relevant STATUS / spec sections; reuse recorded test evidence when the source is unchanged. Run checks invalidated by new edits, and one full suite when shared changes or repository rules justify it, after checking active runs. Start the supported dev server for focused browser verification. No baseline full rerun is required merely to resume unchanged source.

## DO NOT DO

- Do not restart the project.
- Do not rewrite working systems unnecessarily.
- Do not use Git. Do not initialize Git, push, or connect the project to GitHub.
- Do not upload anything (project files or documents).
- Do not download outside assets without explicit approval.
- Do not replace working prototypes with fake placeholders.
- Do not claim prototypes are production assets.
- Do not skip testing.
- Do not assume automated tests mean human playtesting passed.
- Do not jump randomly ahead in the story.
- Do not stop making prototype characters simply because final models are unavailable.
- Do not spend enormous amounts of time trying to make procedural prototypes into final AAA assets.
- Do not edit `builds/v0.1/` or `builds/v0.2/`.
- Do not mark a human-reported problem as fixed-by-a-human until a human has retested it.

## Development Workflow

```
READ → UNDERSTAND → BUILD → PLAY → OBSERVE → IDENTIFY → FIX → TEST → REGRESSION → POLISH → UPDATE DOCUMENTATION → CONTINUE
```

- **Read / understand:** the four documents, then the code you will touch and its tests.
- **Build** the highest-priority item in small, integrated steps.
- **Play / observe:** run it in the browser (or headless with a probe/bot) and look at what actually happens.
- **Test:** add behavioural tests; run `node tests/run-tests.js`; nothing merges with failures.
- **Regression:** check earlier features still work (the suite includes every mission from every checkpoint).
- **Human playtest checklist:** every new major gameplay system gets a short checklist in `STATUS.md`.
- **Update documentation:** `HANDOFF.md` and `STATUS.md` whenever a major system changes, `README.md` when running or controls change, `MASTER_SPEC.md` only when the long-term specification itself changes. Do not duplicate contradictory information.

## Handoff Instructions for a New Claude Session

1. **Before changing anything, read `MASTER_SPEC.md`, `HANDOFF.md`, `STATUS.md` and `README.md`. Inspect the current source tree and existing build. Do not rely on previous chat history.**
2. The files are the source of truth. If something in them seems wrong, check the code and tests, then correct the document.
3. Run `node tests/run-tests.js` first to confirm the baseline (expect all tests to pass; the count is in *Latest Test Results*).
4. Open `index.html` in a browser (or `node tools/serve.js`) and look at the game before modifying it.
5. Respect the standing constraints: local only, no Git, no uploads, no outside assets without approval, frozen builds untouched.
6. Start with *Immediate Next Task*. Keep human findings and automated results separate. Report honestly using the status words in `STATUS.md`.

## Documentation Hierarchy

| File | Role | Changes |
|---|---|---|
| `MASTER_SPEC.md` | What the game is supposed to become. | Only when the long-term specification changes. |
| `HANDOFF.md` | Where development currently is and what happens next. | Frequently. |
| `STATUS.md` | Detailed factual implementation status and testing record (automated and human). | Whenever something is built, fixed or tested. |
| `README.md` | How the project is run and used. | When running, controls or contents change. |

If these documents disagree, inspect the actual source code and tests before deciding which statement is correct.
